	Object-Oriented Programming (OOP)
		"Object-Oriented Programming is an architectural paradigm that conceptualizes software design around self-contained entities called objects, rather than procedural logic. By encapsulating state and behavior within these unified structures, OOP promotes high cohesion, low coupling, extensibility, and robust code maintainability across complex enterprise applications."
	What is an Object?
		"An object is a dynamic, concrete instantiation of a class executed at runtime, structurally allocated within the JVM heap memory. It serves as a runtime entity that encapsulates localized state (attributes) and behavioral logic (methods). Critically, every object possesses a distinct identity, managed via its memory reference, ensuring uniqueness even if its internal state matches another instance."
		
	"A class is a logical, compile-time construct that serves as an abstract blueprint or prototype for object instantiation. As a user-defined data type, it encapsulates the definitive schema for an object's properties (state) and behavioral capabilities (methods). Structurally, a class serves purely as metadata; it consumes no heap memory until it is actively instantiated into a runtime object."

What is a function?
	-------------------
A function is a self defined block which is used for calculation, printing the result or to perform some specific 
operation.


Advantages of Function :
	------------------------
1) Modularity :
------------
Dividing the bigger task into number of smaller tasks where each sub task will perform indepenedent task.
	2) Easy understanding :
---------------------
Once the complex task is divided into number of modules then it is very easy to understand the entire module.
	3) Reusability :
--------------
Based on our requirement we can reuse a particular module 'n'
number of times.
	Note :In java we will always reuse our java classes.
	4) Easy debugging :
-------------------
Each sub task is isolated (different) from another sub task so we can easily find the errors.


Why we pass argument/parameter to the function :
	------------------------------------------------
We pass parameter to a function for getting more information regarding that function. If we don't pass parameter then it may represent partial information.


Why functions are called Method in java ?
	-----------------------------------------
If we write a function inside a class then it is called Method.

**What is platform independency in java ?
	-----------------------------------------
C and C++ programs are platform dependent programs that means the .exe file created on one machine will not be executed on the another machine if the system configuration is different.
	That is the reason C and C++ programs are not suitable for website development.

The role of java compiler :
	• Syntax Verification 
		 Checks the source code for syntax errors. 
	• Semantic Verification 
		 Verifies type compatibility (LHS = RHS). 
		 Checks variable declarations and initialization. 
		 Verifies method calls, return types, and access rules. 
		 Reports compile-time semantic errors. 
	• Bytecode Generation 
		 Converts the .java source file into a .class bytecode file.
	

Role of the JVM (Java Virtual Machine)
	The Java Virtual Machine (JVM) is responsible for loading and executing the bytecode.
	Its main functions are:
		 Loads the .class file into memory.
		 Verifies the bytecode for security and correctness.
		 Executes the bytecode by converting it into machine code that is specific to the operating system and processor.
		 Manages memory, garbage collection, and runtime execution.
	The JVM contains an Interpreter, which executes bytecode instruction by instruction. It also uses a Just-In-Time (JIT) Compiler to convert frequently executed bytecode into native machine code, improving performance.

A Java Enabled Browser (JEB) is a browser that can execute Java applets using a JVM (built-in or through a Java plug-in).


Q. What is the difference between machine code (C/C++) and Java bytecode?
	Machine code(also called native code or object/executable code) generated from C and C++ programs is directly understood and executed by the operating system and the processor. 
	On the other hand, bytecode generated by the Java compiler is understood by the JVM and is not executed directly by the operating system.
	
	Note: The term "bit code" is incorrect in the Java context. The correct term is machine code (for C/C++) and bytecode (for Java).

Strictly speaking, the operating system itself does not "understand" machine code. The CPU executes machine code, while the operating system loads the executable into memory and manages its execution.

An Operating System (OS) is system software that acts as an interface between the user/applications and the computer hardware. It manages hardware resources, loads programs into memory, schedules CPU execution, manages files and devices, and provides services for application programs.

Simpler exam definition
An Operating System (OS) is system software that manages computer hardware and software resources and provides a platform for application programs to run.


			C/C++ Source Code
			        │
			        ▼
			Compiler
			        │
			        ▼
			Machine Code (.exe)
			        │
			        ▼
			Operating System
			   ├── Loads the executable into memory
			   ├── Allocates memory
			   ├── Creates a process
			   ├── Schedules CPU time
			   └── Provides system services
			        │
			        ▼
			CPU
			   Executes the machine instructions

What is the difference between
	
	---------------------------------------------------------------
JDK : 
-----
		
	JDK is used for developing, compiling, debugging, and executing Java applications.
	It contains JRE + Development Tools.
		 javac → Java compiler (source code → bytecode) 
		 java → Java launcher 
		 jdb → Debugger 
		 javadoc → Documentation generator 
		 javap → Class file analyzer
	
	
	JRE :
-----
		 JRE provides the environment required to run Java applications. 
		 It contains JVM + Core Java Libraries. 
		 It can execute Java programs but cannot develop or compile them.
	-------------------------------------------------------------------

JVM :
-----
		 JVM is an abstract virtual machine responsible for executing Java bytecode. 
		 Main responsibilities: 
			 Loads .class files 
			 Verifies bytecode 
			 Manages memory 
			 Executes bytecode using: 
				 Interpreter → Executes bytecode instruction by instruction 
				 JIT Compiler → Converts frequently executed bytecode into native machine code for better performance
		
	JIT Compiler (Just-In-Time Compiler) :
	--------------------------------------
		 JIT Compiler is a component of the JVM's Execution Engine. 
		 Its purpose is to improve Java application performance by converting frequently executed bytecode (Hot Spots) into native machine code at runtime. 
		 The generated native code is stored in the JVM's Code Cache (RAM) and executed directly by the CPU.
		

How does the JVM use the Interpreter and JIT Compiler during execution?
	---------------------------------------------------------------------
		 When a Java program starts, the JVM loads the bytecode and the Interpreter begins executing the bytecode immediately. This allows the application to start quickly without waiting for compilation. 
		 While the program is running, the JVM's profiling mechanism monitors execution and identifies frequently executed code, known as "Hot Spots." These may include frequently called methods or loops that execute many times. 
		 The JVM sends these Hot Spots to the JIT (Just-In-Time) Compiler. The JIT compiler converts the frequently executed bytecode into optimized native machine code specific to the current CPU architecture. 
		 The generated native code is stored in the JVM Code Cache (memory/RAM). When the same code executes again, the JVM uses the compiled native version instead of interpreting the bytecode repeatedly, improving performance. 
	Short interview answer:
		"Initially, the JVM uses the interpreter to execute bytecode quickly. At runtime, the JVM profiler identifies hot code paths. The JIT compiler then compiles those frequently executed bytecode sections into optimized native machine code and stores them in the code cache. Future executions use the native compiled code directly, which improves performance."
	This version demonstrates that you understand the complete flow: Bytecode → Interpreter → Profiling → Hot Spot Detection → JIT Compilation → Native Code → CPU Execution.


What is the difference between Compiler and Interpreter ?
	----------------------------------------------------------
		Feature	Interpreter	JIT Compiler
		Translation	Line-by-line, on the fly	Entire blocks (methods/loops) at once
		Startup	Fast; begins execution instantly	Slower; needs time to analyze and compile
		Execution	Slow; re-translates repeated code	Fast; runs native hardware instructions
		Memory	Low; does not save translations	Higher; saves compiled code in a Code Cache


How many data types in java ?
	------------------------------
	 Primitive and Reference data types. Java provides 8 primitive data types: byte (8 bits), short (16 bits), int (32 bits), long (64 bits), float (32 bits), double (64 bits), char (16 bits), and boolean (JVM dependent). Among these, 6 data types are numeric: byte, short, int, long, float, and double. Apart from primitive types, Java supports reference data types such as classes, arrays, interfaces, and String.

What is the difference between statically typed and dynamically typed language ?
	--------------------------------------------------------------
statically typed language :
----------------------------
The languages where data type is compulsory before initialization of a variable are called Statically typed language.
In these languages, once we define the type of the variable then it will hold same kind of value till the end of the program.
	Example of statically typed languages :
C,C++, JSE, C# and so on
	
	
	Dynamically typed language :
----------------------------
The languages where Data type is not compulsory, It is optional to initialize the variable are called Dynamically Typed Language.
	In these languages we can provide all different kinds of values to the variable during the execution of the program.
	Example of dynamically typed languages :
Visual Basic, Javascript, Python and so on

Flavors of Java :
	-----------------
In java, We have total 4 flavors :
	1) JSE (Java Standard Edition) [Stand-alone Application]
	2) JEE (Java Enterprise Edition) [Websites OR web application]
	3) JME (Java Micro Edition) [Mobile Apps OR Android Appln]
	4) JavaFX (It is used to Develop GUI Application) [outdated]

Description of main() Method:
	-----------------------------
	The main() method is the entry point of a Java application. The JVM starts the execution of a Java program by invoking the main() method.
	
	It is a user-defined method with a predefined signature.
	
	public:
	--------
	Allows the JVM to access the method from outside the class.
	
	static:
	--------
	Allows the JVM to invoke the method without creating an object of the class.
	
	void:
	------
	Indicates that the method does not return any value.
	
	main:
	------
	A predefined method name recognized by the JVM as the starting point of program execution.
	
	String[] args:
	--------------
	Stores the command line arguments passed during program execution.

	-----------------------------------------------------------------

Why the main method of java accepts String array as a parameter ?
	String is a sequence of alpha-numeric character, that means providing more wider scope and the application can convert each string to the required data type. 
	
	String is a universal representation for command-line input. Any value typed on the command line—such as a number, boolean, filename, date, or other text—is received as a string. The program can then convert it to the required data type if needed.
	
	-----------------------------------------------------------------

System.out.println():
	---------------------
	It is a Java statement used to print output to the console.
	
	• System is a predefined class available in the java.lang package that provides access to system resources.
	
	• out is a public static reference variable of type PrintStream that represents the standard output stream (console).
	
	• println() is a predefined method of the PrintStream class that prints the specified data and then moves the cursor to the next line.


	String str = 90 + 90 + "Ravi" + 80 + 80; //180Ravi8080

Command Line Argument :
	------------------------
	The values passed to a Java program through the command line at the time of execution.
	
	These arguments are automatically stored in the String[] args parameter of the main() method as String objects.
	
	By using command line arguments, we can pass different input values at runtime without modifying the program.
	
	Advantages:
	• No need to hardcode input values.
	• The same compiled program can be executed multiple times with different inputs.
	• Useful for passing filenames, numbers, configuration values, etc., during program execution.

IDE (Integrated Development Environment): 
	----------------------------------------- 
	An IDE is a software application that provides a complete environment for software development.
	It combines different development tools like a code editor, compiler, debugger, and build tools into a single application.

What is a Package in java :
	----------------------------
	A package is logically a namespace, and physically it is represented as a folder structure, used to organize related classes, interfaces, and sub-packages into a single group.
	
	1)Improves code organization and makes searching easier. 
	2) Avoids naming conflicts by allowing the same class name in different packages. 
	3) Provides access protection. 
	4) Promotes code reusability.
	

Naming Convention in java :
	----------------------------
Naming convention provides two important characteristics: 
		a) Standard code (industry-accepted coding practice) (not exactly an standard)
		b) Improves readability of the code
	
	
	1) Class Names:
	----------------
	• Use PascalCase (each word starts with an uppercase letter).
	• Should be nouns.
	
	
	2) Method Names:
	-----------------
	• Use camelCase (first word lowercase, following words start with uppercase).
	• Should usually represent actions.
	
	
	3) Variable Names:
	------------------
	• Use camelCase.
	• Should be meaningful names.
	
	4) Package Names:
	-----------------
	• Use lowercase letters.
	• Usually use reverse domain naming style.
	
	5) Constants(Static + final not only final):
	--------------
	• Use uppercase letters with words separated by underscores.
	
	Naming conventions are guidelines, not compiler rules.
	
	camelCase → variables/methods
	PascalCase → classes/interfaces
	UPPER_SNAKE_CASE → constants (static final)
	lowercase → packages

Tokens in java :
	----------------
	Token is the smallest building block of the program that is recognized by the compiler.
	
	Token is categorised into 5 types 
	
	  1) Keyword
	  2) Identifier
	  3) Literal
	  4) Punctuators (Seperators)  
	  5) Operator
	  
	Keyword
		--------
		A keyword is a predefined word whose meaning is already defined by the compiler.
		
		 Even though true, false, and null are literals rather than keywords, they are reserved and cannot be used as identifiers in Java.
		
	---------------------------------------------------------------------
	
	Identifiers :
	
	--------------------------------------------------------------------
	
	Literals :
		----------
		Any constant which we are assiging to variable is called Literal.
		
		In java we have 5 types of Literals :
		
		1) Integral Literal
		2) Floating Point Literal
		3) Boolean Literal
		4) Character Literal
		5) String Literal
		6)null literal
		
		
		================================================================================
		INTEGRAL LITERALS & WRAPPER CONVERSIONS REVISION NOTES
		================================================================================
			 CORE SPECIFICATIONS
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* Definition  : A numeric literal without any decimal point or fraction.
			* Data Types  : byte (8-bit), short (16-bit), int (32-bit), long (64-bit).
			* Default Type: Every whole number literal (e.g., 90) is an INT by default.
			* Pure OOP?   : Java is NOT a pure Object-Oriented language because it supports primitives.
			
			 THE 4 REPRESENTATION SYSTEMS
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			a) Decimal     : Base 10. Default format. Uses digits 0-9. (e.g., 90)
			b) Octal       : Base 8. Starts with a leading '0'. Uses digits 0-7.
			c) Hexadecimal : Base 16. Starts with '0X' or '0x'. Uses 0-9 and A-F / a-f.
			d) Binary      : Base 2. Starts with '0B' or '0b'. Uses digits 0 and 1 (JDK 1.7+).
			
			 INTEGRAL CASTING & COMPILER ASSISTANCE
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* Implicit Casting: Allowed when assigning a default 'int' literal to byte/short, 
			                    PROVIDED the literal value stays within type range.
			* Range Checks    : byte (-128 to 127) | short (-32768 to 32767).
			* Internal Action : The compiler explicitly injects the cast internally, 
			                    e.g., changing `byte b = 12;` to `byte b = (byte) 12;`.
			* Suffix Rules    : Long literals require an explicit 'L' or 'l' suffix.
			
			 GLOBAL CROSS-LANGUAGE COMPARISON
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* Java        : Strict type safety | Default int | Signed only | 'L' suffix for long
			* C / C++     : Supports unsigned types | Automatic sizing varies by CPU architecture
			* Python      : Dynamically sized int | Arbitrary precision (never overflows memory)
			* JavaScript  : No explicit int type | Handled as 64-bit floating-point 'Number'
			
			 CODE EXECUTION PROOFS & TRAPS
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			// --- Base System Traps ---
			int a1 = 018;          // ERROR: Digit 8 is out of range for Octal base 8
			int b1 = 0777;         // Valid: Octal format
			int a2 = 0Xage;        // ERROR: Character 'g' is out of range for Hex base 16
			int z1 = 0B112;        // ERROR: Digit 2 is out of range for Binary base 2
			
			// --- Size & Casting Controls ---
			byte b_err = 128;      // ERROR: Exceeds maximum byte limit of 127
			byte b_ok = 125;       // Valid: Implicitly cast to byte by compiler
			short s_wide = b_ok;   // Valid: Automatic type casting (Widening)
			
			short s_val = 127;
			byte b_narrow = (byte) s_val; // Valid: Explicit narrowing required for variable types
			
			final int x = 5;
			byte b3 = x;           // Valid: Variable is marked final (treated as compile-time constant)
			
			int y_cast = (int) 18L;// Valid: Explicitly downcasting a long literal to an int
			
			// --- Runtime Output Format Gotcha ---
			int literalConversion = 015; // Evaluates Octal 015
			System.out.println(literalConversion); // Prints 13 (JVM automatically outputs as Decimal)
			
			// --- Underscore Visual Separation Rules (Java 7+) ---
			int ok1 = 1_000_000;   // Valid: Underscores strictly between numeric digits
			int errStart = _100;   // ERROR: Cannot locate underscore at the beginning (treated as identifier)
			int errEnd = 100_;     // ERROR: Cannot locate underscore at the end of the literal
			int errRadix = 0x_10;  // ERROR: Cannot place underscore adjacent to number system prefixes
			
			
			 UTILITY: STATIC STRING CONVERSIONS (INTEGER CLASS)
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* Purpose: Used because primitives cannot travel over networks; requires wrapper objects/text.
			* Method 1: Integer.toBinaryString(int x) -> Returns binary text representation.
			* Method 2: Integer.toOctalString(int x)  -> Returns octal text representation.
			* Method 3: Integer.toHexString(int x)    -> Returns hexadecimal text representation.
			
			// Example execution:
			String binStr = Integer.toBinaryString(10); // Returns "1010"
			String octStr = Integer.toOctalString(10);  // Returns "12"
			String hexStr = Integer.toHexString(10);    // Returns "a"
			
		
		================================================================================
		
		
		
		================================================================================
		FLOATING-POINT LITERALS REVISION NOTES
		================================================================================
			 CORE SPECIFICATIONS
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* Data Types  : float (32 bits / 4 bytes) | double (64 bits / 8 bytes)
			* Default Type: Every fractional literal (e.g., 1.2) is a DOUBLE by default.
			* Standard    : Follows IEEE 754 floating-point specification.
			
			 TYPE HIERARCHY & CASTING RULES (IMPORTANT)
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* Widening (Left-to-Right) : Implicit conversion. No casting needed.
			* Narrowing (Right-to-Left): Explicit casting required, else compilation error.
			
			  byte ---> short ---> int ---> long ---> float ---> double
			  [------------ INTEGRAL ------------]   [-- FLOATING-POINT --]
			
			* Note: You CAN assign any integral literal to a float/double variable.
			* Note: You CANNOT assign a float/double literal to an integral variable.
			* Why?: Float/double can hold much larger numbers via exponents and have precision.
			
			 NUMBER SYSTEM LIMITATIONS (INTERVIEW TRAP)
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* Integral literals can be in 4 forms: Decimal, Octal, Binary, and Hexadecimal.
			* Floating-point literals can ONLY be represented in DECIMAL form.
			* Prefix Trap: Adding 0 or 0X to a literal with a decimal point fails.
			
			 REPRESENTATION SUFFIXES & FORMATS
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* 'f' or 'F'  : Mandatory for float literals (e.g., 1.2f).
			* 'd' or 'D'  : Optional for double literals (e.g., 10d).
			* Exponent    : Uses 'e' or 'E' for power-of-10 (e.g., 15e2 = 15 * 10^2 = 1500.0).
			
			 GLOBAL CROSS-LANGUAGE COMPARISON
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* Java        : Strict type safety | Default double | Mandatory 'f' suffix for float
			* C / C++     : Default double | Supports 'f' suffix | Implicit narrowing allowed
			* Python      : Only 'float' data type exists (equivalent to Java's double)
			* JavaScript  : Only 'Number' data type exists (always 64-bit IEEE 754 double)
			
			 CODE EXECUTION PROOFS & TRAPS
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			// --- Suffixes & Casting ---
			float f = 1.2;          // ERROR: Cannot implicitly convert double to float
			float f1 = (float)12.90;// Valid: Explicit casting
			float f2 = 12.66F;      // Valid: Explicit float suffix
			float f3 = 56.89f;      // Valid: Explicit float suffix
			double d1 = 10d;        // Valid: Explicit double suffix
			double d2 = 18D;        // Valid: Explicit double suffix
			
			// --- Exponent / Scientific Form ---
			double e1 = 15e2;       // Valid: 1500.0
			double e2 = 15e-2;      // Valid: 0.15
			double a = 1.5e3;       // Valid: 1500.0
			float b = 1.5e3;        // ERROR: Exponential output is double, needs suffix/cast
			float c = 1.5e3F;       // Valid: Suffix provided
			
			// --- Integral vs Floating-Point Assignments ---
			double d = 10;          // Valid: int implicitly widened to double (10.0)
			int e = 10.0;           // ERROR: Cannot assign double to int
			long f_long = 10D;      // ERROR: Cannot assign double to long
			int g = 10F;            // ERROR: Cannot assign float to int
			long l = 12.78F;        // ERROR: Cannot assign float to long
			
			// --- Number System Traps ---
			double d1_dec = 0.129;  // Valid: Standard decimal notation
			double x1 = 0129.89;    // Valid: Decimals ignore leading 0; treated as decimal 129.89
			double y1 = 0167;       // Valid: No decimal point. Evaluates as OCTAL 167 (= Decimal 119.0)
			double z1 = 0178;       // ERROR: No decimal point. Evaluates as Octal, but '8' is invalid in octal
			double x2 = 0X29;       // Valid: Hexadecimal integer implicitly widened to 41.0
			double y2 = 0X91.5;     // ERROR: Hexadecimal notation cannot have a decimal point
			double d_hex = 0Xdead;  // Valid: Hexadecimal integer implicitly widened to 57005.0
			double e_hex = 0Xdead.0;// ERROR: Hexadecimal notation cannot have a decimal point
		
		
		================================================================================
		
		
---------------------------------------------------------------
		boolean literal :
			-----------------
			It is used to represent two states i.e. either true or false.
			 
			In boolean literal, we have only one data type i.e boolean data type which accepts 1 bit of memory OR depends upon JVM 
			implementation.
			
			// --- C / C++ (Valid) ---
			bool x = 0; // Evaluates to false
			bool y = 1; // Evaluates to true
			
			// --- Java (Compile-time Errors) ---
			boolean x = 0; // Error: incompatible types: int cannot be converted to boolean
			boolean y = 1; // Error: incompatible types: int cannot be converted to boolean
			
			In languages like JavaScript and Python, other data types can evaluate to a boolean state in conditional statements, but they are not Boolean literals.
			• Java: Does not have truthy/falsy values. Only true and false work in conditions.
			• JavaScript Falsy: 0, "" (empty string), null, undefined, NaN, false. (Note: [] is truthy in JS!)
			• Python Falsy: 0, "", None, false, [] (empty list), {} (empty dict).  
			
boolean x = "true";  //error
				boolean y = "false";  //error
		
----------------------------------------------------------------
		================================================================================
		CHARACTER LITERALS REVISION NOTES
		================================================================================
			
			[1] CORE SPECIFICATIONS
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* Data Type   : char (Primitive type, unsigned numeric)
			* Memory Size : 16 bits (2 bytes)
			* Range       : 0 to 65535 (No negative values)
			* Encoding    : UTF-16 Unicode (Supports international characters)
			
			
			[2] THE 5 REPRESENTATION METHODS
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			a) Single Character  : Enclosed in strict single quotes.
			b) Integral Value    : Assigned to int variables to fetch raw ASCII/Unicode value.
			c) Code Point (Dec)  : Direct integer assignment within the 0 - 65535 range.
			d) Hex Unicode       : 4-digit hexadecimal format prefixed by '\u' ('\u0000' to '\uffff').
			e) Escape Sequences  : Special control characters prefixed by a backslash.
			
			
			[3] GLOBAL CROSS-LANGUAGE COMPARISON
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* Java        : 16-bit | UTF-16 Unicode | Strict single quotes ''
			* C / C++     : 8-bit  | ASCII (Usually) | Strict single quotes ''
			* Python      : No primitive char type  | Treats single character as String (str)
			* JavaScript  : No primitive char type  | Treats single character as String
			
			
			[4] CODE EXECUTION PROOFS & TRAPS
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			char ch1 = 'a';       // Valid: stores 97
			int asciiVal = 'A';   // Valid: stores 65 (char to int assignment)
			
			char maxRange = 65535; // Valid: Maximum range allowed
			char overRange = 65536;// ERROR: Possible loss of precision (out of bounds)
			
			char hexChar1 = '\u0041'; // Valid: Hex 0041 is Decimal 65 ('A')
			char hexChar2 = '\uffff'; // Valid: Maximum hex range
			
			char symbol1 = 63;     // Valid: Prints literal '?' (Unicode code point 63)
			char symbol2 = 64;     // Valid: Prints literal '@' (Unicode code point 64)
			
			char escapeN = '\n';   // Valid: Newline character
			char stringErr = "A";  // ERROR: Double quotes create a String object, not char
		
		
		================================================================================
		
		================================================================================
		NULL LITERAL REVISION NOTES
		================================================================================
			 CORE SPECIFICATIONS
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* Data Type   : No specific type. It belongs to a special, unnamable 'null type'.
			* Target Use  : Can be cast or assigned to any reference type (Object, Array, Interface).
			* Memory Size : Represents an unallocated or empty memory pointer address (0x0).
			* Primitives  : CANNOT be assigned to primitive data types (like int, boolean, char).
			
			 MEMORY MANAGEMENT & OBJECT REFERENCING
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* SCP/Heap    : It does not point to any active memory block on the Heap.
			* GC Trigger  : Assigning `null` to a reference breaks its link to the Heap object, 
			                making that object eligible for Garbage Collection.
			
			 GLOBAL CROSS-LANGUAGE COMPARISON
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* Java        : lowercase 'null' keyword | Checked at runtime | Throws NullPointerException
			* C / C++     : 'NULL' macro (evaluates to 0) or 'nullptr' keyword (C++11 type-safe pointer)
			* Python      : Capitalized 'None' keyword | Represents a singleton object of NoneType
			* JavaScript  : 'null' primitive value | Bug gotcha: `typeof null` returns "object"
			
			 CODE EXECUTION PROOFS & TRAPS
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			// --- Valid & Invalid Assignments ---
			String s = null;       // Valid: Assigned to a reference type
			int[] arr = null;      // Valid: Arrays are reference types
			int x = null;          // ERROR: Incompatible types: null cannot be converted to int
			
			// --- Runtime Traps & Exceptions ---
			String text = null;
			int len = text.length(); // RUNTIME ERROR: Throws NullPointerException (NPE)
			
			// --- Comparison and instanceof Rules ---
			System.out.println(null == null); // Prints true (Evaluating raw null states)
			String str = null;
			System.out.println(str instanceof String); // Prints false (null is never an instance of any class)
			
			// --- Explicit Type Casting ---
			String explicitStr = (String) null; // Valid: null can be cast to any reference type
			System.out.println(explicitStr);    // Prints "null" safely
			
			// --- Method Overloading Ambiguity (Classic Interview Trap) ---
			// If you have method(String s) and method(Object o), calling method(null) 
			// compiles and resolves to method(String s) because it is the most specific type.
		
		
		================================================================================
		
		================================================================================
		CLASS LITERALS REVISION NOTES
		================================================================================
			 CORE SPECIFICATIONS
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* Definition  : An expression consisting of the name of a class, interface, array, 
			                or primitive type, followed by a period and the token 'class'.
			* Data Type   : Evaluates to an instance of `java.lang.Class<T>`.
			* Runtime Unit: Represents the metadata of a loaded class within the JVM.
			* Usage Scope : Primarily utilized in Reflection, Logging frameworks, and 
			                Dependency Injection containers.
			
			 REFLECTION METHOD COMPARISONS
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* Class Literal: `String.class` -> Evaluates at Compile-time. Safe (No Exceptions).
			* Object Method: `str.getClass()` -> Evaluates at Runtime. Requires active object instance.
			* Class Loader : `Class.forName("java.lang.String")` -> Evaluates at Runtime. 
			                 Throws Checked Exception (ClassNotFoundException).
			
			 GLOBAL CROSS-LANGUAGE COMPARISON
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* Java        : `ClassName.class` syntax | Generates a java.lang.Class token
			* C++         : `typeid(ClassName)` operator | Generates a std::type_info object
			* Python      : `type(obj)` or directly using class name identifier as an object
			* JavaScript  : Class constructor references | e.g., `String` or `MyClass` identifier
			
			 CODE EXECUTION PROOFS & TRAPS
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			// --- Valid Class Literal Evaluations ---
			Class<String> c1 = String.class;       // Valid: Standard class token reference
			Class<Integer> c2 = int.class;         // Valid: Primitives have class literals
			Class<void> c3 = void.class;           // Valid: Even void keyword has a class literal
			Class<int[]> c4 = int[].class;         // Valid: Multi-dimensional arrays supported
			
			// --- Primitive vs Wrapper Caveat (Interview Trap) ---
			System.out.println(int.class == Integer.class); // Prints false (Primitive != Wrapper metadata)
			System.out.println(int.class == Integer.TYPE);  // Prints true (Integer.TYPE matches primitive int)
			
			// --- Syntactic Compile-Time Errors ---
			Class<?> err1 = "Java".class;   // ERROR: Cannot access .class on an object variable instance
			String path = "java.lang.String";
			Class<?> err2 = path.class;     // ERROR: .class token cannot resolve string variables at compile-time
		
		
		================================================================================
		
		
		================================================================================
		STRING LITERALS REVISION NOTES
		================================================================================
			 CORE SPECIFICATIONS
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* Data Type   : String (Reference/Object type, NOT a primitive)
			* Memory Size : Dynamic (Depends on string length and content)
			* Encoding    : UTF-16 Unicode character sequence
			* Immutability: Once created, the contents of a String object cannot be changed
			
			 MEMORY MANAGEMENT (STRING CONSTANT POOL - SCP)
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* Storage Box : String literals are stored in a special area of Heap memory called SCP.
			* Optimization: JVM reuses existing literals from SCP to save memory.
			* GC Behavior : Objects in SCP are NOT easily collected by Garbage Collection.
			
			 GLOBAL CROSS-LANGUAGE COMPARISON
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			* Java        : Reference type | SCP optimization | Strict double quotes ""
			* C           : Null-terminated character array (char[]) | Mutable by default
			* C++         : std::string class (Object) | Mutable by default
			* Python      : Immutable str type | String interning optimization | '' or "" or """ """
			* JavaScript  : Primitive string type | Immutable | '' or "" or `` (Template literals)
			
			 CODE EXECUTION PROOFS & TRAPS
			--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
			// --- Literal Creation (Uses SCP) ---
			String s1 = "Java"; // Creates 1 object in SCP
			String s2 = "Java"; // 0 objects created; s2 directly references existing "Java" in SCP
			System.out.println(s1 == s2); // Prints true (References the exact same object)
			
			// --- New Keyword Creation (Bypasses SCP enforcement) ---
			String s3 = new String("Java"); // Creates 1 object in normal Heap (SCP already has "Java")
			System.out.println(s1 == s3);    // Prints false (Different memory locations)
			
			// --- Comparison Gotcha ---
			System.out.println(s1.equals(s3)); // Prints true (Compares actual text content)
			
			// --- Compilation Errors ---
			String err1 = 'Java'; // ERROR: Single quotes are strictly for character literals
			String err2 = "Java;  // ERROR: Unclosed string literal
			
			// --- Escape Sequences inside Strings ---
			String path = "C:\\Users"; // Valid: Backslash must be escaped
			String tabs = "A\tB";       // Valid: Embeds a horizontal tab
		
		
	
	================================================================================

	What is a local variable ?
		---------------------------
		If a variable is declared inside the body of method OR block OR Constructor then it is called Local /Automatic/ Stack/ temporary variable.
		
		A local variable must be initialized by the developer before 
		use because local variable does not have any default value.
		
		We can't apply any kind of modifier on local variable except final.
		
		As far as it's scope is concerned, It must be used within the same method body only that menas we can't use local variable outside of the method body/block/constructor.
		
		All local variables are the part of the method body, all the methods are executed in a special memory in java called Stack Memory so local variables are the part of Stack memory.
		
		A local variable must be pre-declared(notice using while loop) and initialized before use. 
		
		
var keyword is allowed only for local variable.
		
		--------------------------------------------------------------
		
		Stack Memory works on LIFO (Last In First Out) basis.
		
		In java, Whenever we call a method then a separate Stack Frame will be created for each and every method.
Once the method execution is over then the corresponding method Stack frame will also be deleted from Stack Area, that is the reason we can't use local variable outside of the method. 
		
		Each stack frame contains 3 parts :
		   1) Local Variable Array
		   2) Frame Data
		   3) Operand Stack

Limitation of if else :
-----------------------
The major drawback with if condition is, it checks the condition again and again so It increases the burdon over CPU so we introduced switch-case statement to reduce the overhead of the CPU.

Switch case statement in java :
	-------------------------------
	It is a selective statement so, we can select one statement among the available statements.
	
	break is optional but if we use break then the control will move from out of the switch body.
	
	We can write default so if any statement is not matching then default will be executed.
	
	[long we can pass in switch case from java 14v]   // not sure what does this means
	
	We can pass String from JDK 1.7v and we can also pass enum from JDK 1.5v.  
	
	<<<<<<<<<<<<<<<<---------------------My-------------->>>>>>>>>>>
	
	Data Types Allowed in a switch Statement in Java:
	In Java, the switch statement supports specific types of data. The case labels must be constant expressions or literals, and the values must be unique.
	✅ Allowed Data Types:
		 byte
		 short
		 char
		 int
		 String (Introduced in Java 7)
		 enum (Introduced in Java 5)
		 Wrapper Classes (Byte, Short, Character, Integer, String) (Because of auto-unboxing)
		
	❌ Not Allowed Data Types:
		 Floating-point numbers (float, double)
			 The reason is that floating-point values may not be exact due to precision issues.
		 Boolean (true or false)
			 switch requires multiple cases, but boolean has only two possible values (true and false), so if-else is preferred.
		 Long (long)
			 switch internally converts values into an integer-based lookup table, but long is 64-bit, making it incompatible.
		 Objects (except String and enum)

Types of Variables in java :
	----------------------------
	In java based on the data type we have only 2 types of variables :
	
	Primitive Variables :
	----------------------
	If any variable in java declared with primitive data type i.e 
	byte, short, int, long, float, double char and boolean then it is called Primitive Variable.
	
	On primitive variable we can't assign null literal as well as with primitive variable we can't invoke a method.
	
	Reference Variable :
	--------------------
	If we declare any variable with reference data type i.e with class name, interface name and so on then it is called Reference variable.
	
	
	Now, Based on the declaration position and modifier, Variables are further divided into four types :
	Class Level (Static filed , Instance or Non-static filed)and Method Level(Local and Parameter var)


===================

Object Oriented Programming (OOPs)
-----------------------------------
"Object-Oriented Programming is a programming paradigm that organizes software design around objects rather than functions or logic. These objects combine state (data) and behavior (methods) to model real-world entities, which ultimately improves code modularity, reusability, and maintainability."


What is an Object ?
--------------------
"An object is a runtime instance of a class that occupies memory on the heap. It represents a real-world entity by combining state (data) and behavior (methods), and it has a unique identity maintained by the JVM."

Object consists of three key characteristics: state, behavior, and identity."

What is a class :
	-------------------
	
	"A class is a logical, compile-time construct that serves as an abstract blueprint or prototype for object instantiation. As a user-defined data type, it encapsulates the definitive schema for an object's properties (state) and behavioral capabilities (methods). Structurally, a class serves purely as metadata; it consumes no heap memory until it is actively instantiated into a runtime object."
	

Advantages of OOPs :
	-------------------
	1) Modularity (Dividing the bigger task into number of smaller task)
	2) Reusability (We can reuse the BLC classes)
	3) Flexibility (Easy to maintain )

Features Of OOPs :
	------------------
	1) Class
	2) Object
	3) Abstraction
	4) Encapsulation
	5) Inheritance
	6) Polymorphism


Instance Variable OR Non static Field :
	---------------------------------------
	Instance variable we can declare at class level.
	
	If a non static variable is declared inside the class but outside of the method then it is called Instance Variable OR Non static field.
	
	Example :
	---------
	An instance variable is automatically created and initialized with default value at the time of Object creation. [We can't even think about instance variable without object creation]
	
	
	An instance variable is used to represent the properties of an object hence without object instance variable will not exist.
	
	As far as it's scope is concerned, An instance variable can be accessible anywhere within the class as well as outside of the class also using valid access modifier
	
	Whenever we create an object, a separate copy of all the instance variables will be created.
	
	
	  
	Role of instance variable OR Non static field (Object Properties) while creating an object.
	
	In java, Whenever we create an object, a separate copy of all the instance variables will be created with each and every object.

----------------------------------------------------------------
What is the role of class variable OR static field in Object creation ?

What is a static field ?
	------------------------
	It is a variable which we should declare at class level.
	
	If we declare a variable inside a class with static modifier then it is called static field OR Class Variable.
	
	
	A static field is automatically created and initialized with default value at THE TIME LOADING THE .CLASS FILE INTO JVM MEMORY.
	
	Whenever we create an object in java then a single copy of static field will be created and the same single copy will be sharable by all the objects.
	
	* It is sharable by all the objects hence if we modify the value
	  by any of the Object reference then it will be mofifiable for
	  all the objects.
	
	
	Note : The main purpose of static field to save the memory.

----------------------------------------------------------------
When we should declare a variable as an instance and we should decalre as static variable ?
	
	Instance Variable :
	-------------------
	If the value of the variable is different with respect to object 
	then we should declare the variable as an instance variable.
	
	Static Variable :
	-----------------
	If the value of the variable will be common with respect to all the objects then we should use static variable.

What is variable shadow ?  (variable hiding)
	-------------------------
	If class level variables and method level variables are having exactly same name then method level variable will hide class level variable inside the method body OR constructor OR block, This concept is known as Variable Shadow.

----------------------------------------------------------------
this keyword in java :
	----------------------
	• Variable Shadowing (Variable Hiding)
		 When instance variable names and parameter (or local) variable names are the same, the local (or parameter) variable gets priority over the instance variable.
		
		 This is known as "Variable Shadowing", where the local variable hides the instance variable.
		
	• How "this" Solves the Problem
		 To distinguish instance variables from local variables, Java provides the this keyword.
		 this always refers to the current object, and since instance variables are part of an object, we can use this to access them.
		
	Limitations of this
		 The this keyword cannot be used inside a static context (like a static method, static block, or static nested class) because "this" requires an object, and static contexts belong to the class, not an instance.

Constructor :
	-------------
	What is the advantage of writing constructor in our class ?
	------------------------------------------------------------
	If we don't write a constructor in our program then variable initialization and variable re-initialization both are done in two different lines.
	
	1. Constructors
	• Junior Phrasing: "A constructor is a special method used to initialize an object when it's created using the new keyword."
	• Senior Upgraded Phrasing:
"A constructor is a specialized initialization construct invoked implicitly during object allocation via the new keyword. Its primary architectural responsibility is to establish the initial valid state of an object and bind mandatory dependencies during runtime instantiation."
	• The Secret "Flex": Mention that “it does not possess a return type because its implicit return is the memory reference of the newly allocated object itself.”
	
	Modifiers on Constructor :(to be removed , Remember these one words anwer , which I already know the concepts I am preparing  only for nailing the interview 
		-----------------------------
		Constructor can accept all types of access modifiers which are applicable for accessibility level 
		like private, default, protected and public.
		
		Note : We can't apply static, final, abstract and synchronized modifier on constructor.
	
	IQ :
	----
	Why we should declare a constructor with private Access modifier ?
		
		1) If we want to declare only static variables and static methods inside a class then we should decalre constructor with private access modifier [Object is not required]
		
		Example : java.util.Arrays and java.lang.Math class has private constructor because It contains only static methods and will not allow anyone to create the object.
		
		2) If we want to develop singleton class, for singleton class we should create only one object by the class itself then we should declare the constructor with private access modifier.       
	
	
	Why compiler is adding default constructor to our class ?
		---------------------------------------------------------
		
		1) Without default constructor, Object creation is not possible in java by using new keyword, if the class does not contain user-defined constructor.
		
		2) As we know only class level variables are having default values so, new keyword will initialize all the non static variable with the support of java compiler with default values as shown below.
	
	
	Types of Constructor :
		-----------------------
		
		1) Default No Argument Constructor [Added by java Compiler]
		
		2) No Argument OR Non parameterized OR Zero Argument 
		   constructor [Written by user without parameter]
		   
		3) Parameterized Constructor.   
		   
		
		Default Constructor :
		---------------------
		Whenever we write a class and if we don't write any type of constructor then automatically a default no argument constructor will be added by java compiler.
		
		The main purpose of this default no argument constructor to accept default values with the help of new keyword and java compiler.
		
		No Argument Constructor :
		-------------------------
		If a user is writing constructor in the class without any parameter then it is called No Argument Constructor.
		  
		In this approach, all the objects will be initialized with same value (as shown in the program) so, it is not a recommended way because every object must have unique values.  
		  
		
		Parameterized Constructor :
		----------------------------
		If we pass one or more argument to the constructor then it is called parameterized constructor.
		
		The main purpose of paramterized constructor to initialize the instance variable of the class with different values.
	

IQ :
----
return keyword added by java compiler :
	---------------------------------------
	Whenever we write a method with void keyword OR we are writing constructor then at the last statement of method as constructor, automatically compiler will add one return statement so the control will return back to the caller method.
	


**What is Data Hiding ?
	----------------------
	Data hiding is nothing but declaring our data members with private access modifier so our data will not be accessible from outer world that means no one can access our data directly from  outside of the class.
	


*** What is Encapsulation 
	--------------------------
	[Accessing our private data with public methods like setter and getter]
	--------------------------------
	Binding the private data with its associated method in a single unit is called Encapsulation.
	
	Encapsulation ensures that our private data (Object Properties) must be accessible via public methods like setter and getter.
	
	It provides security because our data is private (Data Hiding) and it is only accessible via public methods WITH PROPER DATA VALIDATION.
	
	In java, class is the example of encapsulation.
	
	Note : If we declare all our data with private access modifier then it is called TIGHTLY ENCAPSULATED CLASS. On the other hand if we declare our data other then private access modifier then it is called Loosely Encapsulated class.
	

Method return type as a class :
-------------------------------
* Any class name/interface / enum / record we can take as a return type of the method.

--------------------------------------------------------------
What is a Factory Method ?
	--------------------------
	If a method return type is class that means that method will return current object then that type of method is known as Factory Method.


What is Shallow and Deep copy in java :
	----------------------------------------
	Shallow Copy :
	--------------
	In Shallow copy, Only one Object will be created but the same object will be refered by multiple reference variables.
	
	If we modify the object properties by any of the reference variable then original object will be modified 
	
	Deep copy :
	-----------
	In deep copy we will create more than one object.
	
	Here 2nd object will copy the content of first object.
	
	Since, Objects are created in two different memory location so, if we modify the content of one object then another object content will remain unchangd.

------------------------------------------------------------

***Pass by Value :
	-----------------
	Java does not support pointers so java only works with pass by value.
	
	Pass by value means we are sending the copy of orginal data to the method.
	
	My------------------>>>>>>>>>>>>
	Primitive Types (Pass-by-Value)
		○ When passing a primitive variable, a copy of the value is passed.
		○ Changes in the method do not affect the original variable.
	
	Reference Types (Pass-by-Value of Reference)
		○ When passing an object, Java copies the reference (memory address), not the object itself.
		○ Changes to the object's properties affect the original object because both references point to the same object.
	

----------------------------------------------------------

Immutable objects means it will not modify (Un-modifiable) so we if try modify the objects then modification will not effect on the existing object it will create another object in another memory location.

In Java, String class and all the Wrapper classes are immutable so un-modifiable.

My---------------->>>>>>>>>>>>
• Immutable objects cannot be modified after creation.
• They provide thread safety, security, and performance benefits.
• Java String, wrapper classes, and date/time API classes are immutable.


🔹 Why is s class immutable?
	• No setters → Cannot change values after creation.
	• final fields → Once assigned in the constructor, they cannot be modified.

HEAP Memory Management by using Garbage Collector :
	-------------------------------------------------- 
	
	In Java, A programmer is only responsible to allocate the memory in the HEAP area, Memory de-allocation is automatically done by a component of JVM called Garbege Collector.
	

What is Garbage Collector in java 
	---------------------------------------------
	It is a autometic memory management in java. It is used to delete un-used objects from the HEAP memory.
	
	Garbage Collector is a daemon thread, It will scan the HEAP area, 
	identify which objects are eligible for Garbage Collector (Object which does not contain any references) then delete those objects from the HEAP memory so we can create another object inside the HEAP memory.
	
	Garbage Collector internally uses an algorithm called Mark and Sweep algorithm to delete the objects from the HEAP memory.
	
	JVM will give the instruction to Garbage Collector to visit the heap memory if the heap memory is 60-70% filled up. A developer can also call Garbage Collector explicitly by using the following static method of System class
	
	System.gc(); //Calling the Garbage Collector explicitly

How many ways we can make an object eligible for Garbage Collector :
	---------------------------------------------------------------
	1) Assigning null literal to existing reference variable :
	2) Creating an Object inside a method :
	3) Assigning new Object to the old existing reference variable:
	

Memory in java :
------------------
In java, whenever we create an object then Object and its content (properties and behavior) are stroed in a special memory called HEAP Memory. Garbage collector visits heap memory only.

All the local variables and parameters variables are executed in Stack Frame and available in Stack Memory.
And even Method execution of static and non-static method also executed in stack memory
-----------------------------------------------------------------

Passing an Object reference to the Constructor :(Copy Constructor)
------------
The main purpose of passing an object reference to the constructor to copy the content of one object to another object.
Learn more about this ----------->>>>>>>>>>> like why , what is advantage? Etc…

Constructor Overloading :
-------------------------
 multiple constructors in a single class but arguments must be different.

 we can call all the constructors with the help of this() [this of].

**THE FIRST LINE OF ANY CONSTRUCTOR IS RESERVED EITHER FOR this() OR super()

First line of any constructor is used to call another constructor either from same class  OR from super class.

this() we can use only for constructor calling but not method calling.


============================================================



Instance Block OR Instance Initializer OR Non static block :
------------------------------------------------------------
An instance block is a very special block in java which is automatically executed at the time of creating the Object.

Every time we will create an Object then instance block will be executed.

A non static block is executed before the Constructor body execution.

The first line of any constructor is reserved for this() OR super(). 

If a contsructor contains super() as a first line of constructor then that
 constructor 2nd line is reserved for Non static block.

The main purpose of non static block to initialize the non static data member of the class that is the reason It is also known as Instance Initializer, It is also used to provide some common message OR common logic execution for all the Objects.

If our class contains multiple non static blocks then it will be executed according to the order.[Top to bottom]

If a user explicitly writes any non static block inside the constructor body then it will be executed as it is, Here compiler will not perform any action.

We cannot write return statement inside a non static block because all the initializers must be executed normally.

	{
		System.out.println("NSB");
		return; //error //  Cannot return from within an initializer
	}

Non static variable initialization order :
-------------------------------------------
The order of non static variable initialization :

Variable initialization at the time of NSV declaration OR
   Variable initialization inside non static block [Both are having equal priority, variable will initialized based on the order {top to bottom}] 
   [Remaining part : Illegal Forward reference, JVM Architecture]



// Step 1: x = 0 (default) (first )


What is a blank final field in java ?
--------------------------------------
A final field must be initialized by user only once.

If a final non static variable is not initialized at the time of declaration then it is called Blank final field.

A blank final field cannot be initialized inside the default constructor.

A blank final field cannot be initialized inside the method
body.

A blank field must be initialized by the developer expilicitly in the following two places only [Till the Object creation]

	a) Inside a non static Block OR
	b) Inside the constructor body

A blank final field also have default value that means all the class level variables are having default value even the variable is declared as final.

A blank final filed must be initialized in all the constructors available in the class.

class Student
{
  final String name;
  
  public Student()
  {
	  name = "Scott";
  }
   
  public Student(String name)
  {
	 this.name = name;
  }   
}

class BlankFinalFieldDemo1 
{
	public static void main(String[] args) 
	{
		Student s1 = new Student();
		System.out.println(s1.name);
		
		Student s2 = new Student("Smith");
		System.out.println(s2.name);
	}
}

// I can see we have given different name using different 
constructor in same BlankFinalField
------------------------------------------------------------

{
  final String name;
  
	{
		name = "James";
	}
  
  public Student()
  {
	  
  }
   
  public Student(String name)
  {
	 
  }   
}

{
	public static void main(String[] args) 
	{
		Student s1 = new Student();
		System.out.println(s1.name);
		
		Student s2 = new Student("Smith");

	}
}

constructor in same BlankFinalField we can use NSB

Relationship between the classes :
	
	In java, In between the classes we have 2 types of relation :
	1) IS-A Relation
2) HAS-A Relation
	
	We can achieve IS-A relation by using Inheritance Concept.
We can achieve HAS-A relation by using Association Concept.
	
--------------------------------------------------------------
Inheritance (IS-A Relation) :
	------------------------------
	Deriving a new class from the existing class in such a way that the new class will acquire all the properties and features (except private properties) from existing class (Developer1).
	
	The main purpose of inheritance to provide "Code Reusability".
	
	using extends keyword.
	
	By using inheritance, The relationship between the classes would be parent and child.
	According to Java, Parent class is called super class and child class is called sub class.
	
	It provides "tightly couple" relation that means, if we modify anything in the super class then it will automatically reflect to all the sub classes.
	
	It provides hierarchical classification of classes, In this hierarchy, If we move towards upward direction then more generalized properties will occur but if we move towards downward direction then more specialied properties will occur.
	
	Types of Inheritance in java :
		-------------------------------
We have 5 types of Inheritance in java :
		1) Single Level Inheritance 
2) Multilevel Inheritance
3) Hierarchical Inheritance
4) Multiple Inheritance [Not supported by using class]
5) Hybrid Inheritance.
	
	Note : a) Inheritance follows top to bottom approach.
       b) In inheritance, It is always better to create the 
          Object for more specialized class (child class) 
	   

-------------------------------------------------------------

How to initialize the super class properties through sub class object :
	
	We should use super keyword in the sub class as a first line of constructor.
	super keyword always referes to its immediate super class.
	
	Just like this keyword, super keyword (non static member) also we can't use inside static context.
	
	Constructors are not inherited in java, meaning we can't call super class Constructor using sub class reference

super keyword we can use 3 ways in java :
	
	------------------------------------------
1) To access super class variable (Variable Hiding)
2) To access super class method (Method Overriding)
3) To access super class constructor. (Constructor Chaining)

1) To access the super class variable (Variable Hiding) :
	---------------------------------------------------------
Whenever super class variable name and sub class variable name both are same then it is called variable Hiding,
	Here sub class variable hides super class variable.
	In order to access super class variable i.e super class memory, we should use super keyword as shown 

Method Hiding = It is only possible with static method.
Method Overriding = It is only possible with non-static method.

2) To call the method super class (Method Overriding)
	------------------------------------------------------
If the super class non static method name and sub class non static method name both are same (Method Overriding) and if we create an object for sub class then sub class method will be 
executed (bottom to top), if we want to call super class method from sub class method body then we we should use super keyword as shown in the program.

3) To call super class Constructor (Constructor Chaining)
	
	Case 1:
-------
super() : Automatically added by compiler to maintain the 
          hierarchy in the first line of the Constructor. It
	  is used to call default OR no argument constructor 
	  of super class.
	
	---------------------------------------------------------------
	
	Case 2 :
---------
super("Scott") : User has to write explicitly to the first line 
                   of the Constructor. It is used to call 
		   Parameterized Constructor (Pramaterized constructor of super class which accepts a String value as a parameter) of super class.

	-----------------------------------------------------------------
Case 3 :
---------
this()  : Written by user in the first line of constructor. It is 
          used to call no argument constructor of current class
	  OR Same class.

------------------------------------------------------------------
Case 4 :
--------
this("Smith") : It is explicitly written by user in the first
                  line of the constructor. It is used to call 
		  parameterized constructor of current class.

=================================================================

**Why java does not support multiple Inheritance ?
	--------------------------------------------------
Multiple Inheritance is a situation where a sub class wants to inherit the properties two or more than two super classes.
	
	In every constructor we have super() or this(). When compiler will add super() to the first line of the constructor then we have an ambiguity issue that super() of sub class will call which super class constructor 
	
	It is also known as Diamond Problem 
	Final conclusion is we can't achieve multiple inheritance using classes but same we can achieve by using interface [interface does not contain any constructor]
----------------------------------------------------------------

HOW MANY WAYS WE CAN INITIALIZE THE OBJECT PROPERTIES ?
	-------------------------------------------------------
	The following are the ways to initialize the object properties 
---------------------------------------------------------------
1) At the time of declaration :
   
Here the drawback is all objects will be initialized with same value.
---------------------------------------------------------------
2) By using Object Reference :
	Here we are getting different values with respect to object but here the program becomes more complex.
---------------------------------------------------------------
	3) By using methods :
		
		A) First Approach (Method without Parameter)
   ----------------------------------------------    
    Here also, all the objects will be iniatilized with same 
    value.
		B) Second Approach (Method with Parameter)
    -------------------------------------------
    Here the Drawback is initialization and re-initialization both are done in two different lines so Constructor introduced.
----------------------------------------------------------------------
	4) By using Constructor 
		
		 A) First Approach (No Argument Constructor)
   
		B) Second Approach (Parameterized Constructor)
			This is the best way to initialize our instance variable because 
			variable initialization and variable re-initialization both will be done in the same line as well as all the objects will be initialized with different values. 
		C) Third Approach (Copy Constructor)
--------------------------------------
			Here with the help of Object reference (Employee class) we are 
initializing the properties of Manager class. (Copy Constructor)
		d) By using instance block (Instance Initializer)
------------------------------------------------------------------
	5) By using super keyword :
		 //Initializing the properties of super class
